## 4. Mallow Creek

- **Terrain:** a creek running south to the sea, thirty paces wide at the top; the **spur**, an old stone cart track
  off the causeway, along the east bank; marsh to the west. Two **stows**, sixty paces apart: the upper (Seven's),
  the lower (the Tarn's) in the bend, where the channel is deeper on the outside, so its far post stood in a yard of
  water at low slack and needed the punt. A **tear** in the bed under each stow's mouth.
- **The stow** (coast name, after stow-nets): two grey posts eight paces apart with the mouth of a long grey skin
  hung between them; behind it a sleeve ten or twelve paces long, closed at the far end, streaming with the tide;
  works the ebb (water out through its sides frosted); swings on its posts like a vane to work the flood; at slack
  the posts stand and the sleeve lies down on the mud. Sheds rungs uncut on a surge. **With one post cut, the
  freed side of the mouth falls and the sleeve's far end lifts and folds toward the nearest line. With both cut,
  the stow dies at once and goes to a film of ash.** On a standing post it can swing.
- **Evidence:** the upper stow leaned *before* the 10.50 gust reached it (Kiva, Pru). The lower stow turned at
  **12.44** on its standing post with the slack holding (fingers silent; grass upright; Perrin's watch); the water
  turned at **12.51**. Penhale: "Things like that don't hear."
- **Crew:** Yeo (officer; green notebook); **Penhale** (officer's reserve; four Hunts, never on the mud); **Ivey**
  (catcher to the closing crew); the closing crew of four and their own officer (short grey man, unnamed); a
  boatman with two punts.
- **Seven:** watched the ebb two hours. 10.50: the upper stow's near post shed a yard on a surge; **Anwen laid a
  wall flat on the water** on Yeo's word (first live boom; Kiva: "Down a hand"); netted by Ivey; 9.1 drams in the
  net's tin, red; remark the creek's. 11.30 purses set with Penhale ("one fathom, two fathom, three"; his brother
  went to sea). Penhale asked what *nobody* was and laughed. 12.12 slack. Near post cut 12.14 on Kiva's call off
  Jude's heel; Kiva hauled, shut; **Pru called *far edge* once** as the sleeve's far end lifted; Kiva came off the
  line; the fold lay down where she had been. Far post cut 12.24; Pru hauled, shut; stow dead 12.29; upper tear
  closed by 12.40.
- **The Tarn:** near post 12.35 (red-haired blade from the mud; Bridie called and hauled with Penhale's count).
  The far post needed the punt. The second line caught; **Bridie asked Yeo whether the lying sleeve was ground;
  Yeo answered by the book**; Bridie walked out on it and freed the line. 12.44 the stow turned; the sleeve came
  over Bridie. The corner-hand (thin Tarn boy) had the far edge by the book and was hauling. Kiva shouted *far
  edge* from sixty paces; nobody on that half was listening for it. **Penhale went to the near edge by the book**,
  ward under, lifted, took Bridie's arm; **the sleeve's far end came round over his back.**
- **The pass in three (live, first time):** Pru: "If you cut the post, the skin folds… They're the nearest" (Yeo
  holds the cut). Perrin proposes the pass; Trewin: "I'll plant"; **Yeo: "It's mine. On my word. Do it."** Kiva's
  change from rehearsal: Pru's ward is not put under anything; it is set a pace off as **the nearest thing**, and the
  sleeve's far end folds onto it; Pru finishes the fold to a loaf-sized lump; the whole sleeve comes up off the two
  people after it. *Nobody.* Kiva's Ember (declared, single) carries it **six paces low** across the thread to the
  east mud; it begins to wrap her right fist; *nobody* from the air; **Trewin opens on the word**, plants it; it
  writhes once and lies still. Yeo: "Cut." The far post cut from the punt; the stow dies; the sleeve lies across the
  channel as ash. **It held. Penhale was dead under it already.** Under his grey ward, Bridie alive in an inch of air;
  his hand round her left wrist; he had been counting fathoms to keep her still and "stopped at three."
- Ivey opened his fingers and turned him; "He's gone." Carried up the east bank on the punt's floorboards
  (Ivey, Trewin, Jude, the boatman, Kiva left-handed); **Perrin lifted the head of the boards at the spur's lip
  against Aske's card.** Yeo laid her coat over him and closed his eyes.
- **Yeo's slate (read aloud):** closed at one, on the flood; *Lives: one. D. Penhale… went to the near edge by
  the book and was taken under the far end. The girl lived under his ward.* Hurt as §7. *The skin was taken off
  them, on the officer's word, by a pass in three… Called. Nobody holding it twice.* **Remarks: none, to any
  four. "The remark's the book's. And mine. I said it was ground."**
- Coombe (1.10): kneels with Yeo; to Kiva: "It held… Write it… And his. In the first column. Before."
- Bags: Seven's two dragged up the east bank before the flood (Jude and the boatman); the Tarn's first likewise;
  the Tarn's second (under the far post) unhauled, the creek's. Lower tear under the flood; to be closed near one in the
  morning, at the bottom of the night's ebb, with a guard.

## 5. Kiva Rowan at exit

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified**; one door declared at every board by her captain's voice. Mon: stood down
  from the Bench (Chair's condition). Tue: **stance**, declared (the Carry). Wed: **Ember**, declared, single, all
  day, off her own thimble (the Hunt).
- **No crossing this movement. No stack. No three-door. Three clean, unchanged.** The pass used one door of hers.
- **Hand (left):** warm Mon (four nights' sulk), Tue (two mornings), Wed (three mornings). Aske: Thursday the 17th is
  the soonest for a seventh, price said first ("I'll say it twice"). The two fingers as before.
- **New injury: right hand**, a burn of cold across the knuckles and the backs of the first two fingers (from the
  live skin on her Ember's fist). Greased and in linen; "blister tonight and peel by Sunday"; Aske: in a week she'll
  not know it happened. She writes left-handed meanwhile.
- **The Ember** carried a live, dragging load six paces; "more than I'd have said it could… It's tired."
- **Yes/no log (a quarter to seven):** Mon 14th the Inner Lock rail, yes; Tue 15th a stave in the cooper's yard on
  Quay Street, yes; Wed 16th the leg of the reader's desk at the gate, yes. Off-hour touches not written (the
  banister, Mon morning in ch. 24; the bed rail, Wed night).
- **Her book:** a page torn from the back (clean pages) for the Bench rail note — **the first page she has ever torn
  out**; Pru keeps it. A new seven-column sheet begun Wed night: *Before. D. Penhale, catcher… Stopped at three.*
  Her ten sheets of Pike's craft and the pass sheet are **lent to Ivey overnight to copy**, to come back by morning.
- **The disk:** cold (checked on the Carry morning and laid on the new sheet Wed night). No fourth-column entry
  owed (no crossing).
- **The dream:** not recurred. **Known to nobody new.** No fever this movement. White lights: Kiva's alone.
  "Unroofed Sky" and "Breach" do not appear.
- **The Proprietor's letter to Crest:** known to Kiva (Adlam), Orla (her book). She wrote on the right-hand side:
  *I told him to.*
- **Plain words that travelled:** the rail page at the Bench (one water; give it a count of its own); *set*
  at the ladder; *down a hand* to Anwen; *let it come to you* at the pass; to Ivey, the stow turned before the
  water, so whoever has the far edge must watch the thing, not the water; "a stow's a sleeve… The book's for a
  sheet."

## 6. Team Seven at exit

- **Standing: ninth after three boards, 53, one mark off the roll** (§3).
- **Perrin:** captain; **the seventh rib on the left cracked across, not through**; sixth and eighth sprung as before.
  Aske's card (Tue 2 p.m.): *No plant. No lift. Nothing carried heavier than a cup. Strapped, seen morning and night.
  A fortnight at the least.* Stood down from the Hunt; captained from the spur with the glass and watch. **Lifted the
  head of Penhale's boards once at the spur, against the card.** On being envied: "It's not better… It's just the other
  side of the same thing." His ladder: "The ladder was mine." He asked for a second plant on Sunday; it was there.
- **Pru:** stood the Bench (catcher) and the Hunt (catcher, Kiva's second; far edge called once at 12.20; the fold in
  the pass). **All ten fingertips frosted** (capped in linen; blistering). "Don't say *well called*."
- **Anwen:** the drum (two cells at once); the far end of the cloth; **her first wall laid on water** (live). Warned
  Lyle a second and last time Tue evening; visited her Wed night; will teach her to lean from the floor on Thursday.
- **Jude:** drained the jacket; cut two lashings; held the slipped sling loop with two fingers of his **frosted left
  arm** ("not worse"); two clean cuts at Mallow; dragged Seven's bags up the bank; carried Penhale. Keeps the book.
  "That's what it's for. I think… Carrying it up the bank." Will stay on the coast "if she asks." **No draft made.**
- **The book (Jude):**

| Date | Entry | Held |
|---|---|---:|
| Carried, Mon 14th | | 4.16 |
| Mon 14th | The Bench: Pryce 0.16 (drum), Finch 0.09 (catch), Cade 0.06 (quench and box) | −0.31 → **3.85** |
| Tue 15th | The Carry: Cade 0.22 (twenty-two rungs), Pryce 0.09 (cloth), Talley 0.04 (two lashings); Rowan own thimble | −0.35 → **3.50** |
| Wed 16th | The Hunt: Pryce 0.17 (wall on water), Talley 0.10 (two cuts), Finch 0.12 (the fold); Rowan own thimble; Trewin the Tarn's | −0.39 → 3.11 |
| Wed 16th | The four's quarter of 12.6 clean (Board's half 6.30; the Company's quarter 3.15) | +3.15 → **6.26** |

  Check: 4.16 − 1.05 + 3.15 = **6.26.** Every line is on the page ("Held, three and eighty-five"; "Thirty-five. Held,
  three and a half"; "Spent, thirty-nine… The four's quarter, three and fifteen… Held, six and twenty-six").
  **Combed at the gate Wed:** the net's tin 9.1 red (wet on the ebb); the near-post bag 38.0 red (dry); the far-post
  bag **12.6 at trace, clean** (dry). 59.7 in all; **47.1 red-tagged** (Board evidence; entered by Halden's clerk as
  *claimed under the patent*). **Sealed medical, W, plain: 2.0, unopened, bound.** Asked about by Trewin for Bridie's
  legs; Aske: a burn of cold, not a starved hand; plain would do nothing grease won't; **not wanted**. Jude and Perrin
  on what they would have done if it had been (Perrin: "I'd break it… And then I'd have minded it for the rest of my
  life"). Spoken for: a seventh crossing (~0.30) if wanted from Thursday; not entered.

## 7. Injuries and losses (exact)

- **D. Penhale**, catcher, the Saltmere Company: **dead**, Mallow Creek, about 12.45–12.50 on Wed 16th, under the far
  end of the lower stow's sleeve, making the book's near-edge recovery. Back, beard and hair frosted white; his ward
  left grey "in the shape of a ward." Body to the Company yard.
- **Maud Lyle**, the Ropewalk Hold: **her ward is gone, not shut**, entered by a lodger at the left ankle on Skerry
  Pill at about 11.26. Her stance remains. Nothing on the page restores it.
- **Bridie Hebden**, the Tarn (15, catching): both legs below the knee, **a burn of cold**; wet linen; not to stand
  till Friday; a fortnight.
- **Trewin**, the Tarn captain: both feet to the ankle, frost (boots white); basin of water.
- **Pru Finch:** all ten fingertips. **Kiva:** right knuckles and backs of first two right fingers (§5).
- **Perrin:** the seventh rib, cracked (§6). **Jude:** frosted left arm leaned on once; "not worse."
- **The Tarn's catcher** (unnamed): ankle turned on the lock stair Tue; cannot stand on the Longshore.
- Ash lost: ~40 drams of the Ropewalk's shed rung to the flats from Skerry (red, not netted); part of a Halden rung
  past its purse (Bray: the pill's); part of Seven's shed rung under Anwen's first wall; the Tarn's far-post rung,
  unhauled.

## 8. Other people at exit

| Person | State |
|---|---|
| **Yeo** | Officer, Mallow Creek. Answered Bridie "by the book" and carries it ("I said it was ground"). Decided on the pass. Wrote *Remarks: none, to any four*. Wants copies of Kiva's columns for her crew, Ivey first; "at the next Hunt, I'll have a second" (Ivey's words). Watched Jude; said nothing; no draft. |
| **Ivey** (new) | Short, broad, square dark hands; catcher to Yeo's closing crew. Opened Penhale's fingers and turned him. Took Kiva's ten sheets and the pass sheet overnight to copy; will read them to Yeo's crew at six. Means to have a second. |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Officer of Gannet Creek on the Hunt (Old Four; Westerley first); *Lives: none* at one o'clock. At the Company yard with Yeo at four. Advice for the season already spent; none given. Card ends on the 30th; one night left. "That's not advice. That's a thing I know about her." |
| **Pike** | Came to sit at Gannet Creek; at the Company yard: "He did my place, that night… He had nobody at his far edge." Hands close halfway. |
| **Dinah Coombe** | Drill at the causeway; late to Mallow as she said she would be; knelt with Yeo. **Had her page printed Wed night** with a new head line: *After D. Penhale, catcher, the Saltmere Company, at Mallow Creek… who went in by the book.* The Clerk's leave given at eight. Out by five on Thursday to every beat, van and catcher. |
| **Orla Dane** | At the Bench gallery and the Hunt cart. Walked with Coombe from the causeway to the printer. Told Coombe she held Hart's card a season. Read Mon's book. |
| **Mara Rowan** | Mon night with Hart (§9). **Wed night: decided not to read the bank herself — thirty-six hands, taught by the 22nd** (Peverell's fitters, six of the Works', Rusk's men, the Company's closing crews if Yeo lends them); Hart to teach the slack; she will stand on the landing with a watch and say *now*. Has still **not touched a cell**. |
| **Jessamy Hart** | Showed Mara the springs/neaps record. "I'll teach your thirty-six… what quiet feels like." |
| **Peverell** | Knew Penhale's mother (takes in washing in **Ropery Row**, below the high-water line). Began writing names on fault paper. "I'll not be the keeper who stopped the pumps." |
| **Juniper Carrow** | Called *boom* on Skerry. **Shut her stance at five at the gate in front of the whole school: "I'm learning to declare."** Will declare edge only at the Lane. Walking ten paces and back with one door ("loud"). |
| **Maud Lyle** | §7. "It's quiet… There's one hand on the rope now." Asked Anwen to teach her to lean from the floor. |
| **Treen** | Called *run* twice. Took the far edge and the lodger both ("I've two eyes"). |
| **Trewin** | Planted the pass on the word. "It's horrible… Does it [get less horrible]?" Carry 3:12 planting every step. |
| **Bridie Hebden** (named) | The Tarn's reserve from the gate (grandfather's chandlery at Tarnfoot; the soot-cut jar). Caught for the Tarn; asked what happens in the gap; went onto the sleeve on Yeo's answer; lived. |
| **Kellan Renn** | Halden License first on the Bench, third on the Carry, sixth on the Hunt (one remark, his). Cut the lodger. **Read Halden's claim clerk's ledger at the gate: every red bag *claimed, under the patent of 2391*; "nine-tenths of the Hunt."** Told Kiva. |
| **Pettit** | "I'd have gone… I'd expect nothing else of you." |
| **Selka Dray** | Twenty-Eight won the Hunt. Watched the pass through Marten's glass from Brine Reach. "That's three people… I've no rule against three people… I've not changed my mind about you." On two feet. |
| **Brask** | Old Four's protest; dismissed; raised a flat hand to Perrin. |
| **Mr. Adlam** | Delivered the Proprietor's message in the street. |
| **The Proprietor** | Wrote to Ysra Crest on Sat 12th, in his own hand, for the sequence "on any terms she sets." Not seen. |
| **Noa Bexley, W. Polglase** | The gate mast: **a hair on all eight pens at 12.44 on Wed, nobody on the mast**; entered beside Kiva's account of the stow turning at 12.44/45, seven minutes before the water. No line drawn. Polglase: "He's begun to look." |
| **The Clerk of the Measure** | Ran the Bench and the Carry; dismissed Brask's protest; gave Coombe leave to print. |
| **Healer Aske** | Kiva's card; Perrin's card; Pru, Kiva, Bridie, Trewin treated; the plain not wanted. |

## 9. The harbour (Mara and Hart, Mon 14th, 11.15 p.m.; Wed 16th, 10.30 p.m.)

- **The harbour clock, used deliberately (owner item C12):** Hart's book of slacks for the springs dawn slack —
  **8th 6.12, 9th 6.13, 10th 6.13, 11th 6.14** (staff 4′6½″, 4′6½″, 4′5″, 4′2″) — against the gate's table for the
  same mornings, **5.49, 6.38, 7.27, 8.15** (fifty minutes a day). At the springs, at dawn, the basin's slack is
  **held** near a quarter past six while the sea outside walks round the clock; at the neaps the basin walks with the
  table, twenty-three minutes behind it, "like a dog"; the evening slack walks. Every spring since the ninth month.
  Hart: "As if the basin had an appointment… I write what the hand says." **Not explained.** That is why the 23rd
  could be written a fortnight off.
- Mon night (neap): table 11.10 p.m.; quiet at 11.34; ache a little after a quarter past twelve. **No ring at the
  neaps**; the count goes out of the harbour at slack "and I don't know where it goes." Mara's left hand on the rail
  in the quiet; the count came back through it.
- The Harbour Committee's **fourth** letter (Mon): wants to know by Friday whether the Works will stop the bank on the
  23rd. Rusk: not the Works' bank. Peverell: not on anybody's word but her own.
- Wed night: §8 (Mara's decision).

## 11. New names, places and terms (pending owner review)

Checked by search against every manuscript, planning and provenance file in the repository and
`universe/NAME_REGISTRY.md` before use; no collision found: **the Salt Hall** (Bench venue), **the Inner Lock**
(Carry venue; the lockkeeper unnamed), **the Purser** (the Board's dummy), **Holloway** and **Westerley** (circuits),
**the *Mercury*** (Saltmere paper), **Mallow Creek**, **Skerry Pill**, **Brine Reach**, **the spur**, **a stow** (coast
form), **Bridie Hebden** (the Tarn reserve of ch. 17, now named), **Ivey** (Company catcher), **Ropery Row** (low town).
Rejected for collision: Gill, Bond/the Bond, Withy, Hester, Teague, Peg (removed from a draft line), Marches,
Coldharbour, Sail Loft (used for the Meridian loft). Unnamed: the lockkeeper; the Mercury's young man; the Tarn's
catcher; the Tarn's red-haired blade and corner-hand; Mallow's closing officer and boatman; Halden's claim clerk; the
Board's printer; the comb clerks; the third circuit "that nobody could pronounce."

## 12. Departures from the packet and items for attention

1. **Length:** 52,401 words (`wc -w`), inside 50,000–57,000.
2. **Viewpoint:** Kiva **84.0%** (43,961 of 52,348 by section, script count); cutaways **8,387**: Mara 2,274 (two
   sections), Kellan 2,547 (Skerry Pill, two sections), Rhea 1,432, Anwen 1,159, Orla 975. Inside 6,500–8,500 and
   84–87% after two light trims (§0).
3. **Sections:** 52 (44 internal breaks; about 8.4 per 10,000 words; mean about 1,010 words) after joining 22
   intra-scene breaks during the run (§0).
4. **Lyle and Penhale** as planned. Penhale's recovery partly worked (his ward sheltered the girl); the death is
   carried by Yeo ("I said it was ground"), Coombe (the page held back), Ivey, Pike, Rhea, Jude, Kiva. No narrator
   lesson is stated.
5. **The pass held but saved the girl, not the man.** Perrin's foresight (the second plant asked for on Sunday) and
   Kiva's change on the mud ("be the nearest thing") are the movement's turn. It did not close a tear, kill anything
   or mend a bank; strangers did not do it (Trewin had rehearsed once lit, twenty times dry).
6. **Kiva tore a page from her book** (ledger: "no page ever torn out" until now).
7. **The harbour clock (C12)** is now on the page as an unexplained observation (§9), not retired. The 41-minute
   hold (C13) is untouched.
8. **New evidence:** the gate mast's hair at 12.44 with nobody on the mast; the stow turning seven minutes before the
   water; the upper stow leaning before a gust. Publicly: "the worst Hunt in memory," "a bad rift season," "a gifted
   generation in a bad season." Nobody says the reserved words.
9. **New pressure for Movement Five:** the Proprietor's letter to Crest; Halden's claim on the red bags; Dray's lane;
   Carrow on one door; Seven needing two places in the Lane; Coombe's page in every pocket; Ivey's second.
10. **Perrin lifted against his card** once (the boards at the spur). Aske was not present and is not told on the page.
11. **Jude's frosted arm** leaned on at the Carry; "not worse" by his own account; Aske not shown examining it.
12. **Rhea had a beat** as a Hunt officer (Gannet Creek), on the Board's warrant; not in the packet, consistent with it.
13. **Formula measures** (sentence distribution, paragraph proxy, readability, progression and combat density,
    development cadence) were written toward and **have not been measured**. Only words, sections and viewpoint were
    counted, for the records.
14. **Not done:** the Lane; the hearing; the sealed page; the Glory testimony; the Graving as a national board; the
    Slack; the point of light; the hull; any crossing, stack or three-door; any explanation of the Step, the notch,
    the hair, the held slack, the Hoop, the disk or the dream. No villain.

## 13. Dates ahead

Thursday the 17th: **Kiva's seventh crossing possible** (hand warm three mornings; Aske's price first, "twice"); the
**Lane** in brackets from Thursday; Coombe's page out at five; Ivey reads the columns to Yeo's crew at six and returns
the sheets; Anwen to teach Lyle to lean from the floor; the lower tear at Mallow to be closed near one in the
morning at the bottom of the night's ebb (Yeo; whether it was is not on the page). Friday: the Harbour Committee's deadline; Bridie may stand.
**The prediction opens at noon on Wednesday the 23rd** (dawn slack 6.09). Rhea's card ends on the 30th.
